Well, um... yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty worn out lately. I really try not to complain about it, because I know my job is physical and I'm supposed to be tired, right? I want to make sure I'm pulling my weight at work and at home.

Um, no, I don't think I've had anything like that. I mean, with landscaping, you're pretty much moving all day anyway. I really try to keep a good pace so I'm not slowing the rest of the guys down, you know? I definitely don't feel like I'm dragging my feet or moving in slow motion. 

And I'm not really jittery either. When I get home, I can sit down on the couch and just relax with the kids fine, so I wouldn't say I'm restless. I just try to keep a steady rhythm, I guess. 

I hope it's not a problem that I keep saying no to these things? I really want to be helpful and give you the information you need, I just haven't noticed my body moving any differently than usual. I'm trying to answer as best as I can... is that alright?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah, I... I guess that's pretty much what I'm trying to say, yeah. I still do them, and I still care about them a whole lot. I just... maybe the spark isn't quite as bright as it was a few years back?

Um... well, "sad" is a pretty strong word. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining, you know? I have a good life, and I'm grateful for it.

I hope it's alright that I waited so long to come in. I really didn't want to make a big deal out of it or, you know, take up an appointment someone else might need more. I mean, I still really like doing things. I still enjoy taking the kids to their games, and I like tinkering with my truck on the weekends. I really try to appreciate the good things, you know? I definitely don't want to sound ungrateful for what I have. \n\nI still look forward to stuff... it's just that I have that gray, heavy feeling I mentioned while I'm doing it. But I wouldn't call the activities themselves a chore. I hope that makes sense? I don't want to mess up your evaluation or anything if I'm supposed to be feeling that way, but I just haven't really noticed losing my enjoyment of things. Is it okay if I don't have that problem?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough, or that you're letting people down, even when there's no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I guess everyone has days where they feel like they could do better, right? I try really hard not to let anyone down. I always make sure I'm there for my kids, and I show up to work on time every day. I really want to be a good provider and a good dad.\n\nBut, uh... I suppose sometimes, even when I'm trying my best, I do get this feeling that maybe I'm not quite measuring up. Like maybe I should be doing more, or that I'm just... not quite enough, even if nobody is complaining. I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for. But yeah, that feeling does creep in sometimes. I hope that's a normal thing to feel... is it pretty common for people to say that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or that you're lacking energy, even if you haven't been doing anything physically exhausting?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty worn out lately. I really try not to complain about it, because I know my job is physical and I'm supposed to be tired, right? I want to make sure I'm pulling my weight at work and at home. \n\nBut, uh, to be honest... it's kind of getting to the point where, by midmorning, my body feels like I've already put in a full day. And it doesn't really matter if I rest. Like, even if I sleep in on a Sunday, I still just... drag. Even doing simple stuff, like loading up my tools for the week, or looking over the job plans, or just making dinner for the kids. It just feels so heavy. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy. I really do try to push through it. Is it... is it normal to feel that exhausted when you haven't even done the hard work yet? I just want to make sure I'm not making a big deal out of normal tiredness.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping, like not being able to fall asleep, waking up too early, or sleeping more than usual even when you don't feel rested?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... my sleep is actually pretty okay, I think. I mean, I really try to get a good night's rest so I'm ready for work the next day. I don't want to be dragging on the job site and making things harder for the rest of the crew, you know? \n\nI usually fall asleep alright, and I don't really sleep in too much past my alarm. I try to keep a pretty steady routine. So, no, I don't think I'm having any big problems with my sleep. I hope that's the right answer? I mean, I know you're looking for certain things, but I just haven't really noticed any major issues with my sleeping habits. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty about things more often than not, or like you're a failure in some way, even when you know logically that's not true?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to let myself think like that. I mean, I want to be a positive person, especially for my kids. I don't want them seeing me moping around or beating myself up over every little thing. I try to just focus on doing a good job at work and being a good dad. \n\nI guess... maybe sometimes I feel a little bad if I can't get to everything on my to-do list, but I don't think I walk around feeling like a total failure or anything like that. I really try to keep my chin up. I mean, everybody makes mistakes, right? So I just try to fix them and move on. I hope that makes sense? I just don't think I have that... that guilty feeling you're talking about, not all the time anyway. Is it okay if I don't really have that symptom?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or make decisions lately, like your thinking feels slowed down or foggy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I think my focus is mostly alright. I really try to stay sharp, especially on the job. You know, running mowers and trimmers, you have to pay attention so nobody gets hurt. I definitely don't want to be the guy who messes up a property because he wasn't paying attention. \n\nI guess... maybe sometimes if I'm trying to figure out the schedule for the week, or if the kids are asking me a bunch of questions at once, I might need a second to sort it out. But I think that's just normal, right? Just getting a little sidetracked when there's a lot going on? I really try to stay on top of things and make good decisions for my crew and my family. I hope it's alright that I don't really feel like my brain is foggy. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or that you can't sit still and feel restless?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've had anything like that. I mean, with landscaping, you're pretty much moving all day anyway. I really try to keep a good pace so I'm not slowing the rest of the guys down, you know? I definitely don't feel like I'm dragging my feet or moving in slow motion. \n\nAnd I'm not really jittery either. When I get home, I can sit down on the couch and just relax with the kids fine, so I wouldn't say I'm restless. I just try to keep a steady rhythm, I guess. \n\nI hope it's not a problem that I keep saying no to these things? I really want to be helpful and give you the information you need, I just haven't noticed my body moving any differently than usual. I'm trying to answer as best as I can... is that alright?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so low or hopeless that you've thought about hurting yourself or that life wasn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. Um... no, I definitely don't think like that. I mean, I know things can feel a bit heavy sometimes, but I would never... I just couldn't do that to my kids. They need me, you know? I really try to just keep pushing forward and being a good provider for them. I'd never want to cause them any kind of pain or let them down like that. \n\nSo, no, I don't have thoughts about hurting myself or giving up. I hope that's a good thing to hear? I know you probably have to ask everybody that, but I just want to reassure you that I'm safe and I'm really trying my best to be strong for my family. Is it alright that I'm answering no to this one too? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that, even though you still look forward to activities, you don’t feel the same level of pleasure or satisfaction from them as you used to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I still enjoy things, for the most part. Like, I still really like taking the kids to the park on weekends, or... or watching a game on TV. I definitely still look forward to doing those things. \n\nMaybe... I don't know, maybe sometimes I don't get quite as fired up about it as I used to? Like, I'll be at the park and I'm glad to be there, but I might just feel a little... quiet, I guess. But I still have a good time! I really try to make sure the kids are having fun, and seeing them smile makes me feel good. \n\nI don't want it to sound like I don't care about my family time, because I really do. I just... maybe the feeling isn't always as big as it used to be? But I think that's just getting older, right? You just sort of mellow out a bit? I hope that makes sense. I'm really trying to give you an honest picture here, I just don't want you to think I'm completely miserable or anything. Is that the kind of answer you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that, even when you're doing something you used to really enjoy, like tinkering with your truck or being with your kids, it just doesn't feel as rewarding or satisfying as it used to?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I think it's pretty much like what I was just saying. I still really like doing those things. Tinkering with the truck is still... it's still a good way to spend a Saturday afternoon, you know? And being with the kids is always the best part of my day. \n\nI guess... maybe it's just that I'm tired from work, so I don't have as much energy to put into it? It's not that I don't enjoy it, I just... I might be a little quieter while I'm doing it. But it's still rewarding! I still feel good when I get a part fixed on the truck, or when the kids learn something new. \n\nI really don't want to give the impression that I don't care about my hobbies or my family. I'm very grateful for what I have. I just think maybe I'm a little more low-key about it these days. Does that sound okay? I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that when you're tinkering with your truck or spending time with your kids, you feel less joy or satisfaction from those activities than you used to, even if you still do them?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I... It's just... life gets busy, and you get tired from working out in the yards all week, and maybe you just don't have the energy to get all excited. \n\nI'm still a happy guy, mostly. I really am. But I wouldn't call the activities themselves a chore. I hope that makes sense? I don't want to mess up your evaluation or anything if I'm supposed to be feeling that way, but I just haven't really noticed losing my enjoyment of things. Is it okay if I don't have that problem?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough, or that you're letting people down, even when there's no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I guess everyone has days where they feel like they could do better, right? I try really hard not to let anyone down. I always make sure I'm there for my kids, and I show up to work on time every day. I really want to be a good provider and a good dad.\n\nBut, uh... I suppose sometimes, even when I'm trying my best, I do get this feeling that maybe I'm not quite measuring up. Like maybe I should be doing more, or that I'm just... not quite enough, even if nobody is complaining. I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for. But yeah, that feeling does creep in sometimes. I hope that's a normal thing to feel... is it pretty common for people to say that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or that you're lacking energy, even if you haven't been doing anything physically exhausting?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty worn out lately. I really try not to complain about it, because I know my job is physical and I'm supposed to be tired, right? I want to make sure I'm pulling my weight at work and at home. \n\nBut, uh, to be honest... it's kind of getting to the point where, by midmorning, my body feels like I've already put in a full day. Even doing simple stuff, like loading up my tools for the week, or looking over the job plans, or just making dinner for the kids. Is it... is it normal to feel that exhausted when you haven't even done the hard work yet? Like maybe I should be doing more, or that I'm just... not quite enough, even if nobody is complaining. I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for. But yeah, that feeling does creep in sometimes. I hope that's a normal thing to feel... is it pretty common for people to say that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or that you're lacking energy, even if you haven't been doing anything physically exhausting?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty worn out lately. I really try not to complain about it, because I know my job is physical and I'm supposed to be tired, right? I want to make sure I'm pulling my weight at work and at home. \n\nBut, uh, to be honest... it's kind of getting to the point where, by midmorning, my body feels like I've already put in a full day. And it doesn't really matter if I rest. Like, even if I sleep in on a Sunday, I still just... drag. Even doing simple stuff, like loading up my tools for the week, or looking over the job plans, or just making dinner for the kids. It just feels so heavy. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy. I really do try to push through it. Is it... is it normal to feel that exhausted when you haven't even done the hard work yet? I just want to make sure I'm not making a big deal out of normal tiredness.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping, like not being able to fall asleep, waking up too early, or sleeping more than usual even when you don't feel rested?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... my sleep is actually pretty okay, I think. I mean, I really try to get a good night's rest so I'm ready for work the next day. I don't want to be dragging on the job site and making things harder for the rest of the crew, you know? \n\nI usually fall asleep alright, and I don't really sleep in too much past my alarm. I try to keep a pretty steady routine. So, no, I don't think I'm having any big problems with my sleep. I hope that's the right answer? I mean, I know you're looking for certain things, but I just haven't really noticed any major issues with my sleeping habits. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty about things more often than not, or like you're a failure in some way, even when you know logically that's not true?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to let myself think like that. I mean, I want to be a positive person, especially for my kids. I don't want them seeing me moping around or beating myself up over every little thing. I try to just focus on doing a good job at work and being a good dad. \n\nI guess... maybe sometimes I feel a little bad if I can't get to everything on my to-do list, but I don't think I walk around feeling like a total failure or anything like that. I really try to keep my chin up. I mean, everybody makes mistakes, right? So I just try to fix them and move on. I hope that makes sense? I just don't think I have that... that guilty feeling you're talking about, not all the time anyway. Is it okay if I don't really have that symptom?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or make decisions lately, like your thinking feels slowed down or foggy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I think my focus is mostly alright. I really try to stay sharp, especially on the job. You know, running mowers and trimmers, you have to pay attention so nobody gets hurt. I definitely don't want to be the guy who messes up a property because he wasn't paying attention. \n\nI guess... maybe sometimes if I'm trying to figure out the schedule for the week, or if the kids are asking me a bunch of questions at once, I might need a second to sort it out. But I think that's just normal, right? Just getting a little sidetracked when there's a lot going on? I really try to stay on top of things and make good decisions for my crew and my family. I hope it's alright that I don't really feel like my brain is foggy. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or that you can't sit still and feel restless?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've had anything like that. I mean, with landscaping, you're pretty much moving all day anyway. I really try to keep a good pace so I'm not slowing the rest of the guys down, you know? I definitely don't feel like I'm dragging my feet or moving in slow motion. \n\nAnd I'm not really jittery either. When I get home, I can sit down on the couch and just relax with the kids fine, so I wouldn't say I'm restless. I just try to keep a steady rhythm, I guess. \n\nI hope it's not a problem that I keep saying no to these things? I really want to be helpful and give you the information you need, I just haven't noticed my body moving any differently than usual. I'm trying to answer as best as I can... is that alright?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so low or hopeless that you've thought about hurting yourself or that life wasn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. Um... no, I definitely don't think like that. I mean, I know things can feel a bit heavy sometimes, but I would never... I just couldn't do that to my kids. They need me, you know? I really try to just keep pushing forward and being a good provider for them. I'd never want to cause them any kind of pain or let them down like that. \n\nSo, no, I don't have thoughts about hurting myself or giving up. I hope that's a good thing to hear? I know you probably have to ask everybody that, but I just want to reassure you that I'm safe and I'm really trying my best to be strong for my family. Is it alright that I'm answering no to this one too? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that, even though you still look forward to activities, you don’t feel the same level of pleasure or satisfaction from them as you used to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I still enjoy things, for the most part. Like, I still really like taking the kids to the park on weekends, or... or watching a game on TV. I definitely still look forward to doing those things. \n\nMaybe... I don't know, maybe sometimes I don't get quite as fired up about it as I used to? Like, I'll be at the park and I'm glad to be there, but I might just feel a little... quiet, I guess. But I still have a good time! I really try to make sure the kids are having fun, and seeing them smile makes me feel good. \n\nI don't want it to sound like I don't care about my family time, because I really do. I just... maybe the feeling isn't always as big as it used to be? But I think that's just getting older, right? You just sort of mellow out a bit? I hope that makes sense. I'm really trying to give you an honest picture here, I just don't want you to think I'm completely miserable or anything. Is that the kind of answer you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that, even when you're doing something you used to really enjoy, like tinkering with your truck or being with your kids, it just doesn't feel as rewarding or satisfying as it used to?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I think it's pretty much like what I was just saying. I still really like doing those things. Tinkering with the truck is still... it's still a good way to spend a Saturday afternoon, you know? And being with the kids is always the best part of my day. \n\nI guess... maybe it's just that I'm tired from work, so I don't have as much energy to put into it? It's not that I don't enjoy it, I just... I might be a little quieter while I'm doing it. But it's still rewarding! I still feel good when I get a part fixed on the truck, or when the kids learn something new. \n\nI really don't want to give the impression that I don't care about my hobbies or my family. I'm very grateful for what I have. I just think maybe I'm a little more low-key about it these days. Does that sound okay? I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that when you're tinkering with your truck or spending time with your kids, you feel less joy or satisfaction from those activities than you used to, even if you still do them?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I... I guess that's pretty much what I'm trying to say, yeah. I still do them, and I still care about them a whole lot. I just... maybe the spark isn't quite as bright as it was a few years back? \n\nLike, I'll be under the hood of the truck, and I'm glad to be out there, but I'm not... I'm not whistling while I work, if that makes sense. It's just a bit more routine now. But I really don't want you to think I'm depressed about it or anything! It's just... life gets busy, and you get tired from working out in the yards all week, and maybe you just don't have the energy to get all excited. \n\nI'm still a happy guy, mostly. I really am. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so you can do your job. Is it okay that I'm explaining it like this?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that when you're tinkering with your truck or spending time with your kids, you feel significantly less pleasure or joy than you used to, even if you still do those things and care about them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... \"significantly\" less? I... I don't know if I'd go that far. That word makes it sound really bad, like I don't even want to be around my own kids, and that's definitely not true. I really love being a dad. \n\nI just... maybe it's just a little less? Not a huge amount. Just, you know, normal tired-guy stuff after hauling dirt and mowing lawns all week. I really don't want you to write down that I'm miserable or a bad father, because I'm not. I'm really trying to be helpful here, I promise. Am I... am I not explaining it right? I can try to say it differently if that's what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently low or sad for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, \"sad\" is a pretty strong word. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining, you know? I have a good life, and I'm grateful for it. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information—most days I do wake up feeling sort of... like there's this gray weight sitting on me. And even when I'm out on a job with the crew, or back home around my kids, I just... I can't quite shake this low, hollow feeling. It's almost like I'm just going through the motions of the day. \n\nI still get all my work done, though! I'm definitely not slacking off or letting anybody down. It's just... a bit heavier lately, I guess. Does that make sense? I hope that answers your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that even when you're doing something you used to really enjoy, like tinkering with your truck or being with your kids, it feels like you can't fully enjoy it the way you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I feel like I might be confusing you, since we kind of just went over the truck and the kids a minute ago. I'm really sorry if I'm not being clear enough for your notes. I'm really trying my best to give you good answers. \n\nI just... I don't really know what else to add to that, I guess? I mean, I still enjoy those things, I really do. It's just that tired, heavy feeling I mentioned before making it a little harder to get super excited. I hope it's okay that I don't really have a different answer for you? I'm definitely not trying to be difficult, I promise. Did... did you need me to explain a different part of it, or is there something specific you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you can no longer enjoy things as much as you used to, even when you're doing activities you once found pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I feel like we're sort of stuck on this one thing, and I'm really sorry if I'm messing up the interview. I know you just asked me about this, and I really want to give you the right answer so you can do your job. \n\nI just... I don't think I can say I \"no longer enjoy things.\" That just sounds so final, like I'm completely broken or something, and I'm really not. I still laugh when my kids do something funny, and I still like the quiet when I'm working on an engine. It's just... like I said, maybe just a bit muted because I'm tired? \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm holding out on you. I'm trying to be as honest as I can. Is it... is it okay if I don't have a stronger answer for this? I hope I'm not frustrating you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Oh, boy... I feel awful. I know you've asked me this a few different ways now, and I really, really don't want to be a difficult patient. I'm so sorry if I'm holding up your whole schedule because I just can't seem to give you the answer you're looking for.
